## First Milestone

The first mandatory milestone is:

`Linux CLI client pairs with server, creates a NaiveTCP path, establishes a TUN-backed session, and successfully sends traffic through the server.`

Do not treat Android work as the first proof point. Linux is the primary test harness for validating the shared core.

---

## Bootstrap Decisions

- Migrate to a Cargo workspace immediately. Do not continue building out the legacy top-level `server/` crate structure.
- Keep NaiveTCP as the only required transport until the first Linux end-to-end milestone passes.
- Defer peer-sharing implementation even though it is in v1 scope; build it only after base server + Linux client tunneling is stable.
- Keep Android implementation thin initially: QR pairing, VPN service, one connect/disconnect flow, one path if necessary for first proof.

---

## Configuration Conventions

Use TOML for all human-edited configuration/state files in v1.

Server files:

- Primary config path: `/etc/bonded/server.toml`
- Override via env: `BONDED_CONFIG`
- Authorized keys path default: `/var/lib/bonded/authorized_keys.toml`
- Invite tokens path default: `/var/lib/bonded/invite_tokens.toml`

Recommended server config shape:

```toml
[server]
bind = "0.0.0.0:8080"
status_bind = "0.0.0.0:8082"
public_address = "bonded.example.com:8080"
health_bind = "0.0.0.0:8081"
log_level = "info"
authorized_keys_file = "/var/lib/bonded/authorized_keys.toml"
invite_tokens_file = "/var/lib/bonded/invite_tokens.toml"
```

Recommended authorized keys shape:

```toml
[[devices]]
device_id = "android-phone"
public_key = "base64-ed25519-public-key"
added_at = "2026-03-30T00:00:00Z"
```

Recommended invite token shape:

```toml
[[tokens]]
token = "base64url-token"
expires_at = "2026-03-31T00:00:00Z"
uses_remaining = 1
```

Linux client files:

- Config path: `~/.config/bonded/client.toml`
- Private key path: `~/.local/share/bonded/device-key.pem`
- Public key path: `~/.local/share/bonded/device-key.pub`

Recommended Linux client config shape:

```toml
[client]
device_name = "linux-cli"
tun_name = "bonded0"
server_public_address = "bonded.example.com:8080"
server_public_key = "base64-ed25519-public-key"
preferred_protocols = ["naive_tcp"]
private_key_path = "~/.local/share/bonded/device-key.pem"
public_key_path = "~/.local/share/bonded/device-key.pub"
```

Android stores paired server metadata and client keypair in app-private storage. Do not require user-editable config files on Android.

---

## Initial Library Decisions

- Framing buffers: `bytes`
- Async utilities / codecs: `tokio-util`
- Config serialization: `serde` + `toml`
- File watching: `notify`
- Device identity / signatures: `ed25519-dalek`
- QR generation: `qrcode`
- Linux TUN: `tun`
- CLI parsing for Linux/server utilities: `clap`
- Android Rust build: `cargo-ndk`
- Android bridge approach: thin Kotlin JNI wrapper over shared Rust library, exposed to Flutter through platform channels

If these choices prove unsuitable during implementation, update `Key Implementation Decisions` with the replacement and rationale.

---

## Validation Commands

These are the commands the implementation should keep working as the repo evolves.

Phase 1 baseline:

```bash
cargo fmt --all --check
cargo test --workspace
```

Server checkpoint:

```bash
cargo build -p bonded-server
cargo test -p bonded-server
```

Linux client checkpoint:

```bash
cargo build -p bonded-cli
cargo test -p bonded-cli
```

End-to-end checkpoint target:

```bash
cargo test --workspace -- --nocapture
```

Android checkpoint target:

```bash
cd android && flutter pub get && flutter build apk --debug
```

The implementation agent may add more specific test commands later, but these are the minimum expected validation entry points.

---

## Session Update Protocol

At the end of each work session:

1. Mark every touched task as `in-progress`, `blocked`, or `completed`
2. Add concise notes describing what was implemented and what remains
3. Add newly discovered tasks to the relevant phase
4. Record blockers with exact failure mode
5. Record design decisions with dates

This document must be sufficient for another implementation session to resume work without rereading the entire repository.

---

## Architecture Summary

```
[ App Traffic ]
      ↓
[ VPN / TUN interface ]
      ↓
[ Session Layer: virtual conn ID, sequencing, reassembly ]
      ↓
[ Scheduler: assigns packets to paths (naive in v1) ]
      ↓                    ↓
[ Path A: Wi-Fi ]    [ Path B: Cellular ]
  (NaiveTCP)           (NaiveTCP)
      ↓                    ↓
[ Server: reassembles, forwards to internet ]
```

Shared Rust crate (`bonded-core`) contains: session layer, scheduler, transport traits, auth, protocol framing. Server and Linux client are separate binaries depending on this crate. Android client uses the same core via JNI/FFI.

---

## Workspace Structure (Target)

```
/workspace
├── crates/
│   ├── bonded-core/         # Shared: session, scheduler, transports, auth, framing
│   ├── bonded-server/       # Server binary
│   ├── bonded-client/       # Shared client logic (TUN, path management)
│   └── bonded-cli/          # Linux CLI client binary
├── android/                 # Flutter + platform channels (later: Android-specific)
├── server/                  # (migrate to crates/bonded-server)
└── docs/
```

---

## Phase 1: Core Protocol & Shared Library

Build `bonded-core` with the foundational protocol pieces. Everything in this phase is shared between server and all clients.

| # | Task | Status | Notes |
|---|------|--------|-------|
| 1.1 | Set up Cargo workspace with `bonded-core`, `bonded-server`, `bonded-cli` crates | completed | Added root workspace with crates: bonded-core, bonded-client, bonded-server, bonded-cli |
| 1.2 | Define session frame format (connection ID, sequence number, payload, flags) | completed | Added header + payload frame format in `bonded-core::session` using `bytes` |
| 1.3 | Implement session layer — framing, sequencing, reassembly, connection ID tracking | completed | Added `SessionState` with per-connection TX/RX sequence tracking, out-of-order buffering, in-order flush behavior, and connection mismatch/stale sequence validation |
| 1.4 | Define `Transport` trait (async read/write framed packets) | completed | Added async transport trait + protocol kind enum in `bonded-core::transport` |
| 1.5 | Implement NaiveTCP transport (client + server sides) | completed | Added `NaiveTcpTransport` with length-prefixed frame I/O over Tokio `TcpStream` plus connect/from_stream constructors |
| 1.6 | Define `Scheduler` trait (given packet + available paths → chosen path) | completed | Added scheduler trait with path IDs in `bonded-core::scheduler` |
| 1.7 | Implement round-robin scheduler | completed | Basic round-robin selection scaffold + unit test |
| 1.8 | Implement active-standby failover scheduler | completed | Basic active-first selector scaffold |
| 1.9 | Keypair generation and storage utilities | completed | Added Ed25519 `DeviceKeypair` generation plus base64 private/public key serialization + parse helpers in `bonded-core::auth` |
| 1.10 | Invite token generation and redemption protocol | completed | Added `InviteTokenManager` issue/redeem primitives with URL-safe random token generation and use-count decrement semantics |
| 1.11 | Public key challenge authentication on reconnect | completed | Added challenge creation plus sign/verify helpers using Ed25519 signatures for reconnect auth handshake primitives |
| 1.12 | Unit tests for session layer (framing, reordering, reassembly) | completed | Added tests for outbound sequence increments, out-of-order buffering + flush, connection ID mismatch, and stale sequence rejection |
| 1.13 | Unit tests for transports and schedulers | completed | Added async NaiveTCP loopback exchange test and retained scheduler rotation coverage |

Acceptance gate:

- Shared crates build successfully
- Session framing and reassembly tests pass
- NaiveTCP transport can exchange framed packets between two local processes
- Auth pairing primitives compile and have unit coverage

## Phase 2: Server

Build the server binary on top of `bonded-core`.

| # | Task | Status | Notes |
|---|------|--------|-------|
| 2.1 | Server config loading (env vars + config file) | completed | Server loads TOML via `BONDED_CONFIG`/`--config`, falls back to defaults on read failure, applies env overrides for bind/public/health/log/key paths, and now deserializes partial `server.toml` files by filling missing options from defaults |
| 2.2 | Authorized keys file — load, watch for changes, reload | completed | Added server authorized key store loading from TOML plus `notify` watcher callbacks; hardened watcher to ignore non-mutating access events and debounce rapid bursts to avoid self-triggered tight reload loops; server startup pre-creates missing state files/directories so operators only need to provide `server.toml` |
| 2.3 | Accept NaiveTCP connections, perform auth handshake | completed | Added NaiveTCP listener accept loop and line-delimited JSON challenge-signature handshake with authorized-key enforcement |
| 2.4 | Server-side session management (multiple concurrent clients) | completed | Added concurrent session registry keyed by authenticated client key with unique server session IDs and per-connection frame receive loop lifecycle; improved per-session runtime by offloading frame forwarding into sharded worker queues so slow flow forwarding no longer blocks the transport receive loop; heartbeat handling now only consumes empty-payload `FLAG_PING` frames so data frames with incidental ping-bit are forwarded instead of dropped |
| 2.5 | IP packet forwarding — read from session, write to internet (TUN or raw socket) | in-progress | Added user-space internet egress for IPv4+UDP and IPv4 ICMP echo frames: UDP payloads are relayed via `UdpSocket`; ICMP echo requests are relayed via IPv4 ICMP datagram sockets (`socket2`) with echo-id/sequence matching; retains optional upstream TCP relay fallback for non-IP payloads; ICMP echo traffic now uses a dedicated bounded per-session worker queue (128 frames) so ping bursts are isolated from TCP/UDP forwarding. Added TUN runtime in `bonded-server` with config/env-gated `forwarding_mode=tun`, automatic Linux TUN interface provisioning (`tun_name`, `tun_cidr`, `tun_mtu`), automatic iptables NAT/FORWARD rule installation, default-route egress auto-detection from `/proc/net/route`, and best-effort teardown/restoration on process exit. Added usable first data path via `tun_bridge`: naive-tcp client frames are injected into TUN, return packets are routed back to sessions via destination-IP mapping with per-session server sequence generation; websocket listener is currently disabled in tun mode and remains a follow-up. Session 40 adds optional packet-capture diagnostics: when `BONDED_TUNNEL_PCAP_MAX_MB` is set, server logs tunneled packet payloads to a fixed `/var/lib/bonded/tunnel.pcap` file until the configured MB cap is reached, then stops logging further packets. Session 41 adds TCP RST telemetry in `SmoltcpForwarder` (inbound/outbound counters, tuple logs, and unmatched-flow warnings) to identify whether resets originate from client packets or server-side state transitions. Session 42 hardens TCP flow mapping to avoid potential cross-flow return-data mixing: outbound tuple rewrite now detects ambiguous active/pending matches (same client ip+port and server port but different original destination IP), drops ambiguous packets with explicit warnings, and rejects newly established ambiguous tuples rather than routing arbitrarily. Session 43 refines RST summary reporting to log once per 500-count bucket (instead of repeating unchanged counts), reducing diagnostic log spam while preserving trend visibility. Session 44 adds optional raw TCP bridge byte capture (`BONDED_TCP_BRIDGE_DUMP_MAX_MB`) to `/var/lib/bonded/tcp_bridge_dump.bin`, recording timestamp/session/conn/direction/length + payload for both smoltcp→remote and remote→smoltcp directions with a hard size cap, enabling direct byte-level comparison outside the tunnel encapsulation. Session 45 fixes a drop risk in remote→smoltcp TCP forwarding: `send_slice` partial writes now preserve unsent tails in per-connection pending state and retry on later polls instead of discarding remainder bytes. Session 46 replaces the fixed 1 ms smoltcp poll-thread tick with `Interface::poll_delay()`-driven waits plus existing condvar wakeups, cutting idle/backpressure CPU churn without changing partial-write handling. |
| 2.6 | Return traffic — read from internet, write back to correct client session | completed | Added checksum-correct IPv4 response synthesis for UDP and ICMP echo reply traffic, and wired `forward_frame` to return `None` on per-protocol timeout/no-response so tunneled packets are not spuriously echoed |
| 2.7 | Invite token creation (on admin request / startup) | completed | Added startup invite-token bootstrap that reuses existing usable token or creates/persists a new single-use token |
| 2.8 | QR code generation and emission to logs | completed | Added startup pairing payload JSON + terminal QR emission; logs warning and skips QR when `public_address` is not configured |
| 2.9 | Health check endpoint (HTTP) | completed | Added lightweight HTTP 200 `OK` endpoint on configured `health_bind`, started alongside NaiveTCP listener |
| 2.10 | Configurable log verbosity | completed | Startup tracing level now maps from server config `log_level` (with `BONDED_LOG_LEVEL` override) |
| 2.11 | Dockerfile update for new workspace structure | completed | Updated Docker build to target workspace crates, expose app+health ports, and set runtime config/state defaults under `/etc/bonded` and `/var/lib/bonded` |
| 2.12 | Integration test: server starts, accepts connection, forwards traffic | completed | Added integration coverage at two layers: (1) authenticated NaiveTCP framed payload exchange on a live server stream; (2) packet-by-packet smoltcp TCP bridge e2e in `smoltcp_forwarder` tests that starts a real localhost TCP server, drives synthetic IPv4/TCP handshake + data frames through `SmoltcpForwarder::ingest_packet()`, and asserts bidirectional payload flow. Session 39 extends this e2e to MTU-scale payload transfer (1460-byte TCP payload each direction) with SYN MSS option advertisement and sequence-based response reassembly so large-packet behavior remains deterministic under segment reordering/duplication. |
| 2.13 | Rust-only localhost E2E DNS diagnostic harness | completed | Added ignored/manual `bonded-server` integration test that boots `run_server` on localhost, connects using `bonded-client::establish_naive_tcp_session`, injects synthetic IPv4 UDP DNS query packet to `8.8.8.8:53`, and asserts/prints response-path diagnostics |
| 2.14 | Rust-only localhost E2E HTTP diagnostic harness | completed | Added ignored/manual `bonded-server` integration test that boots `run_server` on localhost, resolves `example.com`, drives synthetic IPv4 TCP handshake + HTTP GET over packet relay, and asserts a valid HTTP status line in returned payload |
| 2.15 | Rust-only localhost E2E SMTP diagnostic harness | completed | Added ignored/manual `bonded-server` integration test that boots `run_server` on localhost, resolves `smtp.gmail.com:587`, drives synthetic IPv4 TCP handshake, sends SMTP `EHLO` and `QUIT`, and asserts SMTP reply codes in returned payload |
| 2.16 | UDP flow sessions with idle timeout and async return path | completed | Replaced one-shot UDP forwarding with per-session flow table keyed by 4-tuple; each flow now uses a persistent connected ephemeral UDP socket, stays alive for 4 minutes since last client packet, and forwards all upstream UDP packets back to the client asynchronously while active |
| 2.17 | Status webpage endpoint for live connection state | completed | Added dedicated status listener (`status_bind`, env override `BONDED_STATUS_BIND`) with a live HTML dashboard and `/api/status` JSON endpoint; the page polls REST data every 2s instead of full-page refreshes and now shows per-flow packet counts in both directions for active UDP/TCP connections alongside authenticated sessions and recent ICMP probe outcomes |

Acceptance gate:

- Server starts from config
- Authorized keys reload works
- Pairing token and QR payload generation work
- A Linux client can authenticate and exchange framed traffic with the server

Build the Linux client on top of `bonded-core` + a thin `bonded-client` lib.

| # | Task | Status | Notes |
|---|------|--------|-------|
| 3.1 | TUN device setup on Linux | in-progress | Added Linux TUN initialization in runtime startup using `tun` crate; full root-required end-to-end validation pending integration phase |
| 3.2 | Network interface detection and enumeration | completed | Added interface enumeration via `pnet_datalink` with unit test coverage in `bonded-client` |
| 3.3 | Client config (server address, auth token or keypair path) | completed | Client runtime now consumes configured server address and key paths, with home-directory expansion and on-demand keypair persistence |
| 3.4 | Pairing flow — redeem invite token, register keypair | completed | Client now includes invite token in auth hello; server redeems single-use invite token, persists the client public key into authorized-keys state, reloads store, and continues authenticated session on the same connection |
| 3.5 | Establish NaiveTCP path to server, perform auth handshake | completed | Added client-side NaiveTCP challenge-signature handshake compatible with server protocol and covered by mock-server unit test |
| 3.6 | Capture traffic from TUN → session layer → transport → server | completed | Added async Linux loop that reads packets from TUN and sends framed payloads over authenticated NaiveTCP transport |
| 3.7 | Receive traffic from server → session layer → TUN | completed | Added reverse async loop that ingests framed payloads from transport and writes packets back to Linux TUN via session reassembly |
| 3.8 | Multi-path: establish paths on multiple interfaces simultaneously | completed | Client runtime now establishes multiple authenticated NaiveTCP paths (bounded by detected interfaces) before entering packet loop |
| 3.9 | Failover: detect path death, shift traffic to surviving paths | completed | Packet loop now removes failed active path and continues on surviving authenticated path when send/recv errors occur |
| 3.10 | Integration test: client + server, ping through tunnel | in-progress | Added authenticated client/server integration test in `bonded-client` that performs handshake + framed payload exchange over NaiveTCP; full ICMP ping-through-TUN validation remains pending root-enabled end-to-end harness |
| 3.11 | Integration test: failover — kill one path, traffic continues | completed | Added deterministic multipath integration test that drops path-0 after auth and verifies payload exchange continues over surviving path-1 |

Acceptance gate:

- Client can pair with the server
- TUN traffic traverses the server end-to-end
- At least two paths can be established
- Killing one path does not terminate the session

## Phase 4: Android Client

Build the Android platform layer. The core Rust logic is shared via FFI.

| # | Task | Status | Notes |
|---|------|--------|-------|
| 4.1 | Flutter project setup (Android target initially) | completed | Created Flutter app scaffold at `android/` with Android-only platform (`flutter create --platforms=android`); validated via `flutter test` and `flutter analyze` |
| 4.2 | Rust → Android FFI bridge (bonded-core compiled for Android targets) | completed | Added `crates/bonded-ffi` (`cdylib`/`staticlib`) with stable C ABI wrappers over `bonded-core` session-frame metadata decoding, validated by crate tests and `cargo check --target aarch64-linux-android`; session 46 wired Android Gradle release tasks (`bundleRelease`/`assembleRelease`) to run `scripts/build-android-native.sh`, enabling one-command `flutter build appbundle` Rust+Flutter release builds |
| 4.3 | Platform channel: Dart ↔ Rust FFI | completed | Added Flutter `MethodChannel` (`bonded/native`) plus Android `MainActivity` bridge calling Rust JNI symbol `nativeApiVersion`; app now displays bridge status with graceful fallback when library is not bundled |
| 4.4 | Android VPN Service implementation | completed | Added `BondedVpnService` shell (session/MTU/address/route establish path), foreground/background lifecycle wiring, manifest service registration, and MethodChannel start/stop/status wiring; explicit user-consent launch flow is implemented via native permission callback; VPN loop now submits outbound TUN packets to Rust JNI and polls inbound JNI packets; added paired-server persistence on Android plus a Rust single-path NaiveTCP session worker that redeems invite tokens, persists key material under app storage, bridges outbound/inbound packet queues, and exposes session-health snapshots back to Kotlin/Flutter for UI status updates; paired-server configuration edits and deletion now persist through platform channels, and the home list refreshes after configuration changes; Android 14/15 foreground startup now declares `specialUse` FGS type in the manifest and passes `FOREGROUND_SERVICE_TYPE_SPECIAL_USE` to `startForeground()` to satisfy VPN background startup enforcement; DNS servers (8.8.8.8, 1.1.1.1) added to VPN builder; routing-loop deadlock fixed via `VpnService.protect()` callback from Rust over JNI; `JNI_OnLoad`/`ANDROID_JVM` pattern used to enable protect calls from Rust threads; session snapshot extended with `outboundBytes`, `inboundBytes`, `connectedAtMs`; dashboard UI rewritten with bytes/uptime display, error banner, and improved connect/disconnect button; home screen adds per-server quick connect/disconnect icon buttons and live "Connected" chip; outbound packet loop now uses a bounded pending queue in Kotlin so transient native-session restart gaps buffer packets instead of repeatedly rejecting/dropping them; VPN builder now disallows the app package (`addDisallowedApplication(packageName)`) so control-plane sockets bypass capture, and Android client path setup no longer hard-fails on `protect(fd)=false`; session 47 fixed a release crash on older binaries by clearing pending JNI exceptions when `protectSocketForNative(I)Z` lookup fails before falling back to `VpnService.protect(I)Z`; session 48 adds `@Keep` on `BondedVpnService.protectSocketForNative(int)` so R8 cannot strip or rename this JNI-reflected method in release artifacts; session 49 adds detailed invite-redemption diagnostics (Kotlin `BondedMain` logs + `result.error` details and Rust JNI stderr error emission) so native pairing failures report concrete causes instead of only a generic message; session 50 switches worker heartbeat scheduling from a 50ms polling cadence to a true interval timer and increases heartbeat to 25s, removing idle 20Hz wakeups while preserving keepalive behavior; session 51 adds Rust-side cancellation-token shutdown and a bounded (250ms) worker join timeout so disconnect can return quickly even when transport receive is pending; session 51 also adds explicit client-transport close callbacks on cancellation (with bounded close timeout) so active sockets are shut down before worker exit; session 52 added Android logcat-visible Rust panic/error instrumentation, installed the rustls process `CryptoProvider` in `JNI_OnLoad`, and split `server_address` from `server_resolved_address` so Android can pre-resolve `charter.codingwell.net` to `97.115.185.254:8080` for socket dialing while still using the DNS name for TLS/WebSocket identity; verified on-device via `DebugVpnActivity` with `Transport paths established: count=1`, `transport[0] = WebSocketTLS`, and connected traffic counters; session 53 prevents shutdown-time recovery/network-rebind callbacks from relaunching a native session after `ACTION_STOP`, verified on a physical device against the local server: disconnect now logs worker shutdown and a clean server-side session close without any late `startNativeSession(...)` or protect-failure restart; session 54 now passes paired `supportedProtocols` from Kotlin into the native session startup path, lets ADB pairing save a different `bootstrap_server_address` than the invite-redemption address for split-port local tests, and protects WireGuard UDP sockets with the Android `SocketProtectFn` before connect so the device can request WireGuard without routing the transport back into the VPN |
| 4.5 | Multi-network support (Wi-Fi + Cellular simultaneously) | blocked | Added Android `ConnectivityManager.requestNetwork()`-based path manager that actively requests Wi-Fi/cellular/ethernet networks, derives per-network local bind addresses from `LinkProperties`, and feeds those addresses into the shared Rust client so NaiveTCP paths bind to distinct local IPs; `BondedVpnService` now restarts the native session when the active network set changes and surfaces active-path count back to the dashboard; client transport establishment now degrades to a single connected path when additional paths fail instead of hard-failing session startup. Session 63 tightens Android path reporting so only usable bind addresses count toward active paths, exposes the actual bind-address/path-summary set in `DebugVpnActivity` status output, filters out non-`INTERNET` cellular networks (for example IMS-only paths), and resolves cellular CLAT IPv4 binds via `NetworkInterface` so the live Pixel starts the native session with `bindAddresses=["192.168.1.140","192.0.0.4"]` and `networkPathSummaries=[wifi:192.168.1.140, cellular:192.0.0.4]`. Session 64 added bind-aware QUIC/WireGuard path establishment plus Android logcat diagnostics for each protocol attempt, then re-ran the Pixel/OnePlus devices: path 1 still times out on `h3`, `wireguard`, and `wss` from `192.0.0.4`, so the app is not yet using Wi-Fi and cellular simultaneously for server uplinks. Session 65 narrowed the blocker further: Android now attempts `Network.bindSocket(fd)` for bound Rust sockets and exposes a `tcp_connect_network` debug action that creates sockets via `Network.getSocketFactory()`. Wi-Fi succeeds on both paths, but the cellular network (`192.0.0.4`) fails in both cases with `EPERM (Operation not permitted)`, so the current blocker is Android/cellular network permissionability for that tracked network rather than missing per-path bind propagation inside Bonded. Build 182 hardens establishment further after a field failure: a phone whose path snapshot led with a stale bind address (192.168.200.99, a network it had left) could not connect at all, because a path-0 failure aborted session startup even with healthy paths behind it. `establish_transport_paths` now returns the established transports paired with their true path indexes and succeeds with any non-empty subset — no single path is load-bearing — and `establish_single_transport_path` pre-flights each bind address with an OS bind check so a dead address fails its path in milliseconds instead of burning per-protocol timeouts. The Android worker builds its supervisor path map from the returned indexes, so missing paths are re-dialed and join later if the network revives. Regression tests: `dead_first_path_does_not_veto_session`, `all_paths_dead_fails_establishment`. |
| 4.6 | QR code scanner screen | completed | Added `mobile_scanner` package with QRScannerScreen widget; includes Android camera permission request handling, camera preview, QR detection, duplicate-scan suppression during navigation, and JSON payload parsing |
| 4.7 | Pairing flow UI — scan QR, redeem token, store keypair | completed | Added `ServerPairingPayload` model, `PairingService` MethodChannel wrapper, and `PairingConfirmScreen` with server details display and redemption UI; updated QR parser to accept server-emitted `server_public_address` (with legacy `public_address` fallback) and reject payloads missing required fields, plus regression tests in `android/test/pairing_model_test.dart`. Session 61 renamed the trusted pairing key surface to `serverIdentityPublicKey` across Dart/Kotlin storage and method-channel boundaries while preserving backward-compatible reads of legacy `serverPublicKey` payloads and prefs data. |
| 4.8 | Connection status dashboard UI | completed | Added `DashboardScreen` with VPN toggle, status display, and connection details; added `HomeScreen` listing paired servers; updated main.dart with named routing for all screens. Session 61 extends the dashboard with trust/transport and peer-sharing cards, and the Android Rust/Kotlin session snapshot now exposes `activeTransport`, total transport count, and active peer-relay count for live status reporting. Build 185 (2026-10-27) supersedes this layout: the dashboard becomes the read-only Session details page and connecting moves to the connect-first main screen — see the build-185 note in the October 2026 log. |
| 4.9 | Server configuration screen | completed | Added ServerConfigScreen showing server details, supported protocols display, and save/delete actions; integrated with HomeScreen PopupMenuButton for server management |
| 4.10 | Background operation | completed | Completed MethodChannel/EventChannel wiring for `startBackgroundVpn`/`stopBackgroundVpn`/`isBackgroundVpnRunning`; `BondedVpnService` now supports Android foreground-service mode with persistent notification and emits background lifecycle events consumed by Dashboard UI |
| 4.11 | End-to-end test: Android → server → internet | completed | Host-side FFI smoke tests pass (50/50 including bind-address assertion); `libbonded_ffi.so` built for arm64-v8a and x86_64 and placed in jniLibs; debug APK builds successfully; device validation is complete on a physical Android device using the release APK in two modes: (1) remote WSS validation via `DebugVpnActivity`, where Kotlin pre-resolves `charter.codingwell.net` to `97.115.185.254:8080`, Rust dials the resolved address while preserving the hostname for TLS/cert-proof verification, and logcat shows `transport[0] = WebSocketTLS` with `state=connected`; (2) local-server validation via `adb reverse tcp:8000 tcp:8000`, a local `bonded-server` running on `127.0.0.1:8000`, and a new ADB-triggerable pairing path in `MainActivity` that redeems an invite token and stores a paired server non-interactively; the real device then connects to the local server with `transport[0] = NaiveTCP`, `state=connected`, and live TCP/UDP flow counters visible on the server status endpoint. Session 55 additionally validated direct device-to-devcontainer LAN reachability for `:443`, `:8000`, and `:51820/udp`, fixed two bootstrap-listener bugs (`close_notify` missing on TLS REST responses and POST body truncation before JSON routing), and re-ran device-side WireGuard startup until Android reported `transport[0] = WireGuard` with `state=connected`. Session 56 closed the remaining server-observed WireGuard gap: client `WireGuardTransport` now treats `boringtun` `TunnResult::Done` as handshake-needed/non-fatal in both send and handshake-initiation paths, and the server WireGuard forwarder mode no longer waits on a nonexistent TUN return channel when `tun_bridge` is absent. After restarting the server and reconnecting the physical device, `GET /api/status` now reports `sessions_count=1` with the Android client key present. |
| 4.12 | Android in-app protocol stress diagnostics page | completed | Added Flutter `StressTestScreen` with configurable EDNS host/resolver, HTTP/HTTPS/HTTP3 targets, and round count; wired a new native `TEST_PROTOCOL_STRESS` action through the existing MethodChannel/foreground-service path; implemented concurrent per-round EDNS UDP queries with explicit OPT/EDNS options, cleartext HTTP, HTTPS, and Cronet-backed QUIC/HTTP3 probes with aggregated in-app log summaries. Build 181 (user-directed) adds a UDP-only probe to the same screen (`TEST_UDP_PROBE` action, `UdpProbeTester`): free-form host/port fields, sends QUIC version-negotiation trigger datagrams over a raw `DatagramSocket` and classifies the outcome — version negotiation received (QUIC endpoint reachable, offered versions listed), other datagram, ICMP port unreachable, or silence — ending in a plain-language verdict so UDP fault localization (phone vs NAT forward vs server listener) can be done from the handset alone, e.g. comparing a known-good QUIC host against the server on the same network |
| 4.13 | Per-path debug controls: long-press pause / reset (branch `path-pause-reset`) | completed | User-directed 2026-10-29: long-press a home-screen path row for Pause/Resume and Reset. Pause keeps the path connected (heartbeats, binding, inbound untouched) but moves it to last resort in outbound flow assignment (`order_candidates_for_send` in bonded-ffi: healthy paths keep hash rotation, paused paths append after; all-paused degrades to today's rotation so the session never blackholes). Reset closes that path's transport and reports the loss to the F4 supervisor, which re-dials + binds it in place; on a single-path session there is no supervisor, so emptying the transport set errors the session for a platform restart. Plumbing: `PathControl` channel from JNI (`nativePausePath`/`nativeResetPath`) through `BondedVpnService` companion + `MainActivity` channel (`pausePath`/`resetPath`) to the worker loop; `paused` flag flows through the snapshot (Rust JSON → Kotlin `SessionPathStat` → Dart `PathStat`) and shows as a Paused chip. Pause is keyed by durable path index and cleared on reset. New unit tests for the ordering helper pass; bonded-ffi suite 14/1 (the 1 is the known rustls CryptoProvider panic); `flutter analyze` clean; Android-target check clean. Bench proof on the OnePlus (WAN h3 session, paths 0 Wi-Fi / 1 cellular): pause path 0 mid-ping moved uplink traffic to path 1 (its outbound growth ~5x while path 0's fell to heartbeat/QUIC-ACK level) and `paused=true` surfaced in the snapshot; resume restored it; reset path 1 dropped transportCount to 1 and the supervisor re-dialed + bound it (~3 s, `restored by supervisor total=2`) with the session's connected-at stamp unmoved. Known limitation: pause steers uplink only — the server pins downlink flows independently, so replies still arrive on a paused path. Verified via temporary `DebugVpnActivity` actions (the OnePlus cannot render Flutter UI); those actions were dropped from the branch after verification, and the bench phone still runs the build that contains them. Merged to main 2026-10-29 as `1d8c1f6` (branch commit `71f2085`) and uploaded to Play internal as v196 (track read-back: internal name=v196 status=completed). |
| 4.14 | Dead-transport blackhole on session restart (Wi-Fi-drop VPN failure) | completed (bench-verified, Play internal v198) | User report 2026-10-29: VPN stops working (ping fails) when Wi-Fi disconnects despite cellular, and reconnects are unreliable without killing the app. Root cause, verified link by link on the OnePlus: every session restart stops the old worker, but its QUIC transports "closed" by finishing the send stream only — no CONNECTION_CLOSE ever left the phone, because (a) the first fix's `closed()` wait resolves locally once the close is queued (bench: close "completed" in 1 ms over a 200 ms-RTT link) and the worker runtime dies before the driver polls again, and (b) a transport whose interface is already dead cannot send a close at all. The server therefore kept dead transports in the session router until the QUIC idle timeout (~30–60 s), and the router's per-flow hash sent replies to them; the handler's mpsc send succeeded (handler still alive) and the frame buffered into the void — the router's retry only covers channel closure, not connection death. Heartbeats bypass the router (direct PONG), so the session looked healthy while data blackholed; server status page proved the forwarder sent pings and got replies the phone never saw. Fix, three layers (server deployed; code committed 2026-10-29 as 54322c4): (1) server redelivery — a failed response-frame send now removes that transport and requeues the frame (bounded: one removal per failure); (2) client close flush — `QuicTransport::close` now finishes the stream, calls `connection.close()`, then sleeps 50 ms so the driver actually flushes the CLOSE before the runtime dies (bench: closes 52 ms, server ends the transports within ~1 s); (3) session epoch — a fresh worker stamps its binding PINGs with `FLAG_SESSION_EPOCH` + an 8-byte start-time epoch, and the server's `SessionOutboundRouter::adopt_epoch` retires every other transport on a strictly newer epoch (supervisor re-dials never carry it; same/older epochs are no-ops; new client vs old server falls back to a plain PING on binding timeout; old clients keep the old behavior). Bench proof on the OnePlus (WAN h3): Wi-Fi kill under a sustained 40-ping went from 32 lost to 40/40 (one 426 ms reply at the rebind, then cellular RTTs); Wi-Fi return 10/10; three rapid disconnect/reconnect cycles 10/10. Server first deployed to dell as image `localhost/bonded-server:epoch`, superseded the same day by the pathset image (epoch is now the rollback). Tests: bonded-core 39/39, bonded-server router 11/11 (new `router_adopt_epoch_retires_previous_worker_transports`). Committed 2026-10-29 as 54322c4 with 4.15; Play internal v198, status=completed. |
| 4.15 | Surgical path-set updates: no session restart on interface change | completed (bench-verified, Play internal v198) | User direction 2026-10-29: when Wi-Fi dies, the cellular connection must NOT be recreated ("doesn't feel elegant"). The session restart was a sledgehammer for a path change; the redesign keeps the worker and surviving transports alive and only adds/removes the paths that changed. Design: (a) the worker tracks desired paths as `path_index -> bind address` (seeded for all requested paths at start, shared with the supervisor via `Arc<RwLock<...>>` since the session-start config is frozen); a `PathControl::PathsChanged` message (same channel family as pause/reset, via new JNI `nativeUpdatePaths` + Kotlin companion `updatePaths`) diffs the new bind-address list by address (`diff_path_addresses`, pure + unit-tested: vanished addresses removed, new addresses take the lowest free index so a returning interface reuses its old index, order changes and duplicates ignored, unbound paths never removed); (b) removed paths are closed (flush close), retired server-side, and forgotten by the supervisor; added paths are dialed by the supervisor with backoff (its `PathsUpdate` channel carries added/removed indexes; `note_added` dials at once, `note_removed` forgets; the supervisor's dial reads the live address map, and `RedialOutcome::Bound` carries the dialed address so the worker can discard a re-dial that went stale mid-dial — with a `path_lost` feedback so the supervisor re-dials the current address instead of marking a discarded path Present); a session that started single-path gets a supervisor spawned mid-session when it grows to two paths; (c) server path retire: the surviving worker sends `FLAG_PATH_RETIRE` + the dead path's server-observed address (from its PONG stamps) over a surviving QUIC/WSS frame transport (WireGuard carries no session frames), and the router's `retire_by_peer` drops that peer's transports at once (peer map recorded at `add_transport`; retired handlers idle out harmlessly, as with epoch retires); without this the kept-alive session would reintroduce the 4.14 blackhole server-side. (d) Kotlin `handleNetworkPathChange` now tries the surgical update first (serialized under `pathUpdateLock`, re-reading the fresh list so the last callback wins; changes during startup park in `pendingPathUpdate` and drain when startup completes) and falls back to the old stop/start rebind only when no live worker answers; no TUN re-handoff on the update path. Empty direct set still errors the session for a platform restart (an idle session is reaped server-side anyway, so waiting out zero paths cannot recover). Bench proof on the OnePlus (bench APK v197, server image `localhost/bonded-server:pathset`, rollback: compose backup `docker-compose.yml.bak-2026-10-29-pathset` → `epoch` image): Wi-Fi kill under a sustained 40-ping: 40/40, 0% loss; phone log shows exactly `path-set change removed=[0] added=[]` with NO rebind and NO new session; server log shows `retired transports for dead path at client request, peer=<cellular 172.56.205.129:4454>, dead_peer=<Wi-Fi 192.168.1.1:47707>, retired=[3]`; NO new cellular auth anywhere (4454 untouched through both events) — the cellular QUIC connection provably survived. Wi-Fi return: `added=[(0, "192.168.1.250")]` (index reuse), supervisor re-dial + restore, total=2; server: new Wi-Fi peer 192.168.1.1:60361 authenticated to the SAME session 0; return ping 10/10. Post-return spreading: 8 TCP flows split 4 fast (connect ~0.025 s, Wi-Fi) / 4 slow (connect ~0.08–0.20 s, cellular). Diagnostic note from the bench: ICMP flows all hash to one path per destination (`flow_hash` has no ports for ICMP — src/dst/proto only), so ping RTTs alone cannot prove spreading; TCP source ports are the spreading probe. Tests: bonded-core 39/39, bonded-ffi 17 passed / 1 failed (known rustls CryptoProvider panic, standing backlog), bonded-server router 12/12 (new `router_retire_by_peer_drops_only_matching_transports`), bonded-client 44/7 (known sandbox UDP/QUIC set). Committed 2026-10-29 as 54322c4 with 4.14; Play internal v198, status=completed. |
| 4.16 | Multipath peer relays + equal transport treatment | completed | User direction 2026-10-31: peer connections stay conceptually separate from paths, but a peer must get EVERY path from its peers (one relay leg per provider path, not today's single upstream), and all transports — peer or not — must be treated equally on a client in load balancing and failover, including future changes. Design: (a) provider claims: the accept task holds per-consumer path claims (path index + claim expiry); a leg claims the lowest provider path not claimed by a live-or-newer claim, so a newer introduction generation takes over a path from an expired/older leg while same-generation legs take successive paths; a leg with no free path is rejected with `no_path` (cap now counts distinct consumers, not legs); the relay task pins its upstream to its claimed path via `establish_single_transport_path` with a bind override re-read from the provider's live path set on every re-establishment, and releases the claim on task end (promptly when the peer connection is observed closed, else at introduction expiry); (b) consumer dial-until-reject: one introduction opens legs sequentially until the provider answers `no_path`; each leg is its own `PeerRelay` transport; leg-0 failure keeps the old error path, later-leg failure keeps the legs already won; renewal re-dials the full set, so the leg count converges to the provider's current path count within one introduction cycle; (c) equal treatment in the worker: flow-spreading candidates are ALL transports (the direct-only helper is deleted so no future change can reintroduce the exclusion), and standby heartbeat eviction (2 consecutive ping failures, ~20 s) applies to relays exactly as to direct standbys (their expiry/closed-connection retirement stays as well); pause stays path-index-based (relays have no path index, and the UI gives them no menu); (d) UI: a separate peer-connections list (home + details) fed by a new per-transport `peerId` in the snapshot; the path lists stay direct-only. Verified 2026-10-31: cargo check clean (client/ffi/server); bonded-core 39/39, bonded-client 48 passed / 7 failed (the known sandbox UDP/QUIC set), bonded-ffi 17 passed / 1 failed (standing rustls CryptoProvider panic), server router 12/12, flutter analyze clean; bench APK built (debug, com.bonded.bonded_app v199, 211,772,595 B, aapt2-verified) after two Gradle failures traced to the dead localhost egress proxy 127.0.0.1:18888, restored via ~/workspace/.tooling/local-proxy-18888.py (forwarder to the working upstream proxy; no TLS termination). Committed 2026-11-02 (d92bce9, with 4.17) + Play internal v199 (track read-back: internal name=v199 status=completed). On-device two-device multipath test still open. Do NOT run cargo fmt --all on this repo (no rustfmt config; an early fmt run reformatted unrelated files and had to be reverted). |
| 4.17 | Provider re-advertise on path change (peer path changes fully handled) | committed + Play internal v199 | User direction 2026-10-31 ("fully handled"): closes the gap found in the 4.16 Q&A — the provider's QUIC listener was bound to one IP chosen at startup and its mDNS advertisement registered once, so if the advertised interface went away, consumers redialed a dead endpoint until the provider restarted. Design: (a) the accept loop is extracted into `run_peer_accept_loop`, one loop per bound endpoint; (b) an advertise watcher (5 s poll) re-resolves the advertise IP from the CURRENT path set and interfaces — the live path map feeds the path-subnet preference, not the session-start config, so a move to a new subnet re-selects onto the new link; pinned configs (explicit advertise IP / explicit bind address) resolve to the same IP forever, so the watcher is a natural no-op for them; no bindable IP keeps the current advertisement; (c) on change: bind the new endpoint FIRST (reusing the original certificate, so the fingerprint consumers pin never changes; a failed bind keeps the old endpoint), then shut down the old mDNS registration and register the new one under the SAME instance nonce (same peer identity; shutdown precedes start because the same instance name would probe-conflict), spawn a new accept loop for the new endpoint, and prune loops whose IP is neither the new one nor still assigned — surviving endpoints keep serving their existing legs undisturbed; a failed registration restores the previous advertisement and the next poll retries; (d) the runtime no longer keeps an endpoint clone (pruning must actually tear endpoints down); the watcher owns the loop set, runtime Drop aborts the watcher, whose Drop aborts the loops. The initial selection also uses the live path set when supplied, so the first advertisement already matches the watcher's steady state. Verified 2026-10-31: cargo check clean; bonded-client 50 passed / 7 failed (same known sandbox UDP/QUIC set; +2 new tests: `select_advertise_ip_for_binds` path-subnet preference, `keep_accept_loop` prune predicate). Not bench-proven on device: a provider path-change scenario needs two devices plus interface changes. Committed 2026-11-02 (d92bce9) + Play internal v199 (track read-back completed). Device bench test (two devices + interface changes) still open. |
| 4.18 | VPN uses the server's DNS when the tunnel is up | committed + Play internal v199 | User direction 2026-10-31. Before: the app hardcoded 8.8.8.8 + 1.1.1.1 as VPN DNS servers and the server had no DNS proxy at all. Design: (a) new server `dns_proxy` module: UDP packets addressed to the tunnel gateway 10.8.0.1:53 are intercepted in `SmoltcpForwarder::dispatch_udp` (before the internet relay) and resolved by the server host's own resolvers from /etc/resolv.conf — in order, one fresh ephemeral socket per resolver, 2 s timeout each, first answer wins; total failure returns a SERVFAIL (ID + question count echoed) so clients fail fast instead of timing out; replies carry per-client-tuple next-sequence exactly like the UDP flow pump; (b) client: `Builder.addDnsServer("10.8.0.1")` ONLY — no public fallback by design, since a fallback would leak queries around the server whenever tunnel DNS is slow or down; (c) scope: UDP only (TCP fallback on truncated responses is a follow-up), smoltcp data plane only (the kernel-TUN forwarding mode is a separate path), IPv4 resolvers only (IPv6 nameserver lines skipped). DEPLOY ORDERING (critical): the server proxy must be deployed to dell BEFORE any client build carrying the DNS change reaches a device — on the current pathset image 10.8.0.1 answers nothing, so that device's DNS would black-hole. The bench APK carries the client change; bench DNS proof is now unblocked by the server deploy and waits on a v199 client bench test. Verified 2026-10-31: cargo check clean; server `dns_proxy` tests 4/4 (resolv.conf parsing, SERVFAIL shape, SERVFAIL frame via `answer()`, stub-resolver forward skipped in-sandbox where UDP sending is EPERM-blocked — probe-skip, same environmental class as the QUIC set); full server bin suite 87 passed / 4 failed (ICMP echo, UDP echo, 2 WireGuard — all need UDP/ICMP sending, sandbox-blocked, pre-existing class); core 39/39, ffi 17/1 (known rustls panic), client 50/7, flutter analyze clean. Committed 2026-11-02 (46aa0db server, efab4a8 client) + Play internal v199 (track read-back completed). Server deployed to dell 2026-11-02 as localhost/bonded-server:dns (release binary from 71bb976, SHA 409b8e25, compose backup docker-compose.yml.bak-2026-11-02-dns, rollback to pathset); health 200, status API live, 1 session re-established within a minute of restart. DNS end-to-end bench test (v199 client through the tunnel) still open. |
| 4.19 | Parallel protocol race, QUIC-first preference, no rotation | committed (13bc003) + Play internal v205 | User direction 2026-11-02 ("initiating all at the same time", "keeping the most preferred h3, wireguard, wss", "not rotating, there is no need to spread the type"): establish_single_transport_path no longer tries protocols sequentially in a per-path-rotated order. Design: (a) every configured protocol is initiated at once, in the sanitized list order with NO rotation — every path uses the same preference, since traffic spreading happens across paths regardless of transport type; (b) the winner is the most-preferred protocol that succeeds (new generic race_preferred helper): a rank-0 success wins immediately, a less-preferred success is held as provisional while a more-preferred attempt is still pending, up to a 5 s grace (PROTOCOL_RACE_GRACE) so a blackholed preferred protocol costs at most 5 s extra instead of its full establish timeout, and the provisional wins at once when no better-ranked attempt can still arrive (checked after both successes and failures); remaining attempts are dropped (cancelled) and logged as "transport attempt superseded"; (c) preference order is now h3 > wireguard > wss everywhere: ClientConfig default, sanitize fallbacks, Android FFI session default, and the relay-upstream fallback (now h3, wss); a configured list order is still honored as preference (rotation is deleted, not the list); (d) preserved semantics: per-attempt 8 s timeouts, the session-start identity gate, and fail-closed on ANY attempt's identity mismatch (even if another protocol connected); per-path errors still join into the path failure string. Verified 2026-11-02: cargo check clean (client/ffi/core/server); 5 new race_preferred unit tests pass (rank-0 immediate win, better-wins-within-grace, grace-expiry-takes-provisional, all-fail + identity, early-exit skips grace wait); bonded-client 55 passed / 7 failed — the 7 are IDENTICAL to the pre-change baseline (verified by stashing the change and re-running: 3 h3_bootstrap + 2 relay QUIC tests are sandbox-UDP-blocked, and the 2 multipath-mix tests already failed on the clean tree — multipath_can_mix_websocket_and_naive_tcp never enables the naive_tcp debug transport so sanitize filters it out and the server task waits forever on its naive accept, and mixed_websocket_quic_failover needs sandbox-blocked QUIC); bonded-ffi 17/1 (standing rustls panic), bonded-core 39/39. Committed 13bc003; uploaded to Play internal v205 (track read-back: internal name=v205 status=completed); device bench test open (QUIC-first on a real phone, and the 2 s-RTT per-attempt 8 s caps are unchanged — a separate tightness). |
| 4.20 | Android TV support (Leanback) for Chromecast | committed (cf06947) + Play internal v208 | User wants Bonded on their Chromecast with Google TV; Play reported it incompatible. Root cause: no TV declaration — MainActivity's intent filter had only the phone LAUNCHER category, so Play treated the app as phone-only. Ruled out from the shipped v205 debug APK + sources: minSdk 24 vs the Chromecast's Android 12 / API 31, arm64-v8a ABI present, required features only faketouch + wifi (camera explicitly not-required), xlarge screens, targetSdk 36. Fix (cf06947): LEANBACK_LAUNCHER category alongside LAUNCHER on MainActivity + android.software.leanback with required=false (phones unaffected); no banner this round — installability only, TV home-screen polish is a follow-up. Verified: debug APK aapt2 lists leanback-launchable-activity for MainActivity (package com.bonded.bonded_app, versionCode 208); release AAB bundletool 208, SHA 67836cdaa1a7527a463c816eeec5cc26ee594b52abf5be585b28ae164cf69b28; uploaded to Play internal v208 (track read-back: internal name=v208 status=completed). Build note: bundleRelease failed twice when the Gradle daemon vanished mid-packaging (no crash log, no OOM entry); fixed by running no-daemon with capped heap/workers (gradle.properties in GRADLE_USER_HOME: daemon=false, Xmx2g, workers 2). Open: Play actually offering the app on the user's Chromecast (confirm on device; internal updates take minutes to propagate); TV UX unverified (D-pad through a phone UI; pairing on a camera-less TV is the open problem — phone-pairs-TV QR proposal in dated log 2026-11-02). Update 2026-11-02: Chromecast still reported incompatible on v214 despite a clean merged manifest (leanback + camera both not-required, minSdk 24, arm64 present); cause identified as the Play Console Android TV form-factor opt-in (user action, not code), with a post-opt-in release needed for TV review. Banner follow-up DONE for v215: drawable-xhdpi/tv_banner.png (1280x720, Converge mark + Bonded wordmark, derived from play-icon-512) + android:banner on MainActivity, uploaded to Play internal v215 as the first post-opt-in release. |
| 4.21 | QR pairing: phone pairs a camera-less client (TV) | committed + Play internal v214 | Problem: a TV has no camera (cannot scan a server QR) and D-pad typing a server address + token + key is miserable. Design (user-approved 2026-11-02): the TV SHOWS a QR code; a phone with the app scans it and pairs the TV with one of the phone's existing servers. TV QR payload (JSON, distinguished by kind): {kind:"bonded-pair-device", version:1, code (128-bit random, base64url — never typed, only scanned), hosts:[TV LAN IPv4s], port:48117, device_label, device_key (FULL key, base64 — corrected during implementation: a fingerprint alone cannot let the phone mint a bound token; the fingerprint is the first 8 chars of the key, shown on both screens for user confirmation)}. TV listener: Dart HttpServer on 0.0.0.0:48117 (dart:io bypasses Android's platform cleartext policy, so plaintext LAN HTTP works with no manifest change), POST /pair {code, server_public_address, server_identity_public_key, invite_token, supported_protocols}; code equality check, close after 5 bad codes, single-use, 5-minute lifetime; on success the TV redeems via the existing PairingService + storePairedServer + select + home. Phone flow: "Pair a device" entry -> scanner in pair-device mode (accepts only kind-bonded-pair-device QRs) -> confirm screen (TV label + fingerprint + server picker from getPairedServers) -> mint -> deliver (try hosts in order, 5 s each) -> "sent, finish on the TV". Mint: new POST /v1/bootstrap/mint-invite in bootstrap.rs, signed envelope mirroring diagnostics (MintInviteRequest {device_public_key, target_device_public_key, requested_at_unix, signature}; signed_payload = JSON minus signature; server checks ctx.authorized_keys + verify_signature, returns 200 {"invite_token"}). Server mint helper in invite_tokens.rs: issue_bound_token, 10-minute expiry, 1 use, bound to the TV key, persisted to the same TOML the handshake redeem path reads live. Binding is REQUIRED, not optional: LAN delivery is plaintext HTTP, so a sniffed token must be useless to anyone but the TV. Expiry enforcement added ONLY for bound tokens (fail-closed, unparseable = expired); legacy unbound tokens behave exactly as before (is_usable = uses > 0 only), since the codebase never enforced expires_at on redeem. InviteToken gains bound_device_public_key (serde default None = legacy unbound); redeem(token, presented_key) returns WrongKey WITHOUT consuming on a bound mismatch (else an attacker could burn the TV's token); all 3 handshake sites pass hello.public_key_b64. Client mint: bonded-client mint module mirroring the diagnostics uploader (keypair.public_key_b64, sign_bytes, request_bootstrap_json over scheme candidates, first Ok wins, parse invite_token). FFI: nativeMintInvite(server, serverKey, storageDir, targetKey) -> token string (load-only keypair; missing = "not paired" error); nativeDevicePublicKey(storageDir) -> b64 (load-or-create; the device keypair is global per storage dir, so the TV identity predates pairing). Kotlin: "mintInviteToken" + "getDevicePublicKey" MethodChannel handlers (filesDir as storageDir, same as redeem). QR rendering: qr_flutter (new dep) on the TV screen; scanning reuses mobile_scanner. Verified 2026-11-02: core 42/42; server 92 passed / 4 failed (the 4 are the pre-existing sandbox set: 2 server_integration relay + 2 wireguard); client 57 passed / 7 failed (exactly the pre-change baseline set: 3 h3_bootstrap + 2 peer-relay QUIC + 2 rotation-era multipath-mix; +2 new mint tests pass); ffi 17/1 (standing rustls panic); flutter analyze clean; flutter test 12/12 (6 new pair-device model tests); debug APK built (versionCode 209, SHA a64aa660adde5a9cc08c3e0bfbbeb8e0cb8a5dcff557389135809cae6f5df95b, aapt2 confirms leanback-launchable). Open: device bench (TV + phone on one LAN); commit + Play push (need the user's word; standing commit rule applies when pushed). Follow-ups: mint rate-limit; TV UI D-pad polish; duplicate delivery after success gets connection-refused (listener is single-use) — harmless edge, documented. |
| 4.22 | Local traffic mode (LAN bypasses the VPN) | committed + Play internal v216 | User direction 2026-11-02, following the investigation the same day. When on, BondedVpnService replaces the single 0.0.0.0/0 route with the complement CIDR set of all IPv4 space except the subnets the device is directly attached to (from ConnectivityManager LinkProperties at establish time); the physical interfaces' own connected routes win by longest-prefix match, so LAN traffic never enters the TUN. Android's Builder has no exclusion API, hence the complement. Pure-Kotlin interval-subtraction + interval-to-CIDR code in LocalRoutes.kt (no Android dependency, JVM unit-tested). Design decisions: every attached subnet counts as local (including the cellular carrier range, which holds no internet destinations); the tunnel's own 10.8.0.0/24 is never excluded (TUN does not exist yet at establish time); routes are fixed at establish, so joining a new network needs a reconnect; DNS stays tunneled at 10.8.0.1 by design, so LAN-by-IP works but router-only hostnames still do not resolve; IPv4-only (no IPv6 routes exist today, so IPv6 already bypasses). Setting: AppSettingsStore allow_local_traffic + Dart switch in Settings (Traffic routing section), applies on next connect. Verified: LocalRoutesTest 8/8 + existing unit tests 2/2, flutter analyze clean, flutter test 12/12, debug APK builds (aapt2 leanback + banner intact). Open: device bench (LAN IP reachable with VPN up, internet still tunneled, new-network-join behavior). |
| 4.23 | Per-app VPN exclusion (bypass list) | committed + Play internal v216 | User direction 2026-11-02. Settings gains an Excluded apps picker (launchable apps via intent query, no QUERY_ALL_PACKAGES permission); checked packages are passed to Builder.addDisallowedApplication at establish time, so their traffic goes direct, unprotected. Deliberately distinct from the reverted own-package exclusion: Bonded itself is never listed and is filtered out again in the channel and the service, so control-plane traffic stays routable through the VPN; uninstalled packages are skipped silently. Setting: AppSettingsStore excluded_app_packages (StringSet) + Dart picker screen with search, applies on next connect. Verified with the 4.22 build (same suite: unit 10/10, analyze clean, flutter test 12/12, debug APK builds). Open: device bench (excluded app traffic direct, everything else tunneled). Android Auto fix (2026-11-11 evening, Tom-reported: could not choose Android Auto in the exclusion list): root cause — `getLaunchableApps` (`MainActivity.kt`) listed apps only via `queryIntentActivities(ACTION_MAIN + CATEGORY_LAUNCHER)`, and Android Auto (`com.google.android.projection.gearhead`) has no launcher activity (it activates when the phone connects to a car), so it never appeared. Fix: a `NON_LAUNCHER_EXCLUDABLE_APPS` list in `MainActivity` (Android Auto first) is probed directly via `packageManager.getApplicationInfo` and appended to the picker (missing packages skipped silently on `NameNotFoundException`, deduplicated against launcher results, own package still filtered, list still sorted by label); Android Auto is declared in the manifest `<queries>` block so the lookup can see it on API 30+ without QUERY_ALL_PACKAGES. The exclusion itself needs no change — `addDisallowedApplication` accepts any installed package. Kotlin unit tests pass; NOT uploaded to Play, NOT pushed. Live check: Android Auto appears in the Excluded apps picker and, once checked, its traffic goes direct on next connect. |
| 4.24 | TV faketouch filter fix (Sabrina / Chromecast) | committed + Play internal v217 | User report 2026-11-02 evening ("supposedly Sabrina is not supported because our app requires faketouch") — verified true. Google's TV hardware docs list faketouch (touchscreen emulator) as unsupported on TV devices and state a required declaration prevents install/launcher appearance, so Play filtered the app from every TV device including Sabrina. This corrects the v205-era assumption that faketouch was present on the Chromecast, which was never verified against device features. Fix: AndroidManifest.xml declares touchscreen + faketouch required=false alongside leanback required=false (phones unaffected — required=false only widens eligibility). Verified on the v217 bundle via aapt2 badging: both features listed not-required, neither in the required set; leanback + banner intact. | 4.28 | IPv6 capture into the tunnel (fail-closed) | Play internal v224 (status=completed) + DEPLOYED to Dell (localhost/bonded-server:ipv6gate, 2026-11-04) | User direction 2026-11-04: capture IPv6 into the tunnel; if it fails, it fails — but it must not go around the VPN. Before: the Android TUN was IPv4-only (10.8.0.2/32, v4 routes, v4 DNS), so on a dual-stack phone IPv6 traffic escaped via the physical network while Tailscale's dual-stack TUN kept it inside. Fix (client + server, no protocol change): (a) `BondedVpnService.kt` adds `fd00:10:8::2/128` (ULA mirroring the v4 10.8.0.0/24 numbering; gateway fd00:10:8::1) plus `::/0` in full-tunnel mode, or the IPv6 complement in local-traffic mode; DNS stays 10.8.0.1 (v4, working via the proxy) — no v6 DNS is added, since AAAA answers already come back over the v4 query path. (b) New `Ipv6Routes.kt`: `Ipv6Cidr` (BigInteger 128-bit) + `excludeIpv6Subnets`, mirroring the v4 interval-subtraction + minimal-CIDR algorithm; new `connectedIpv6Subnets()` mirrors the v4 attached-subnet enumeration. (c) Server `SmoltcpForwarder::ingest_packet` gains an explicit version-nibble gate: non-IPv4 packets are dropped before dispatch (the old code dispatched on byte 9, which for IPv6 is address bytes and could misroute a v6 packet to the IPv4 handlers; the parsers rejected it in practice, but the gate makes fail-closed explicit and tested). Captured v6 packets traverse the tunnel and die at the server gate — they cannot leak, and dual-stack clients fall back to IPv4 through the tunnel. Deliberately NOT done: a client-side v6 drop at the FFI queue (would save tunnel bandwidth; follow-up), and real IPv6 forwarding (NAT66/server v6 egress — a separate project). Tests: `Ipv6RoutesTest` 7/7 (whole space, everything excluded, single /64, host /128, overlap merge, host-bits cleared, formatting round-trip) + `LocalRoutesTest` 8/8 still green; Rust `ingest_packet_drops_ipv6_without_dispatch` passes (worst case: byte 9 reads TCP); full server suite 99 passed / 4 failed (same pre-existing sandbox UDP set). Shipped: app commit `3a99b67`, AAB 92,066,935 B SHA `6b3692f8…`, Play internal v224 status=completed (bundletool-confirmed versionCode 224). Server binary 16,439,648 B SHA `0c0474e4…` from the same commit, image `localhost/bonded-server:ipv6gate` (`879b1b3b9229`), compose backup `docker-compose.yml.bak-2026-11-04-ipv6gate`, rollback = re-point compose at `dotrst` + up -d. Deploy hiccups, both recovered: a VM replacement mid-rsync left only the Dockerfile on Dell (binary missing) — re-staged under `~/workspace/` (not /tmp) and re-rsync'd, SHA re-verified; the compose sed first missed because the image line is indented — corrected to the exact indented match, diff confirms a single-line change. Deploy verification: container up on ipv6gate, health 200, listeners 8080/8081/8082 TCP + 51820 UDP, binary SHA inside the container matches, clean startup (cert valid 69 days); 0 sessions ~1 min after restart — devices idle at ~1:40am, not a fault (the gate only touches packet dispatch after a session exists). Open: device bench (real phone: confirm no v6 leak, measure Chrome Happy Eyeballs fallback delay); GitHub push (commits local only). | Follow-up to 4.26 at the user's word: dial-first alone leaves a phone SYN to 10.8.0.1:853 hanging ~5 s (the gateway route blackholes, so the dial only resolves on timeout) before the RST. Fix (server-side only, `smoltcp_forwarder.rs` + `TUN_DOT_PORT` in `dns_proxy.rs`, no app/protocol change): `dispatch_tcp` rejects a SYN to 10.8.0.1:853 before any live-flow or dial handling, sending a new `PollCommand::RejectSyn` that the smoltcp thread answers with the same crafted RST shape as DialFailed (seq=0, ack=SYN seq + 1 + payload, correct checksums) via a shared `emit_syn_rst` helper — the DialFailed arm now calls the same helper. No dial entry is ever created for the tuple, and no SYN-ACK is sent. Scope is exactly 10.8.0.1:853 (SYNS only; non-SYN packets to the tuple are still dropped); other gateway TCP ports still go through dial-first. Test: `gateway_dot_syn_gets_immediate_rst_without_dial` (RST|ACK, seq 0, ack ISN+1, no SYN-ACK, and `dial_pending_len() == 0` synchronously after ingest, proving no dial was held). Full server suite: 98 passed / 4 failed — the 4 are the same pre-existing sandbox UDP set. Deployed 2026-11-04 ~00:53 EST at the user's word: binary SHA 89642254…, image f7c511a31d52, compose backup docker-compose.yml.bak-2026-11-04-dotrst, rollback = re-point compose at dialfirst + up -d. Verified: container binary SHA matches the build, listeners 8080/8081/8082 TCP + 51820 UDP, health 200, status live, clean startup (16 authorized devices, cert valid 69 days). Note: no client sessions had re-established ~2.5 min after restart and no connection attempts appear in the logs — devices idle at this hour, not a server fault (the change cannot block session setup, which precedes any tunnel dispatch). Live phone-side check CONFIRMED 2026-11-04 ~00:58 EST by the user: a phone SYN to 10.8.0.1:853 RSTs immediately. Pushed to tsuckow2/bonded 2026-11-04. | User report 2026-11-03: 10.8.0.1 "responds" on TCP 853. Root cause: the smoltcp forwarder SYN-ACKed every TCP flow before dialing upstream, so any TCP port on 10.8.0.1 (and any unreachable destination) appeared open at handshake level; only UDP/53 on 10.8.0.1 is genuinely answered (DNS proxy). Fix (server-side only, `smoltcp_forwarder.rs`, no app/protocol change): a SYN for a new tuple is held in a `dial_pending` map while a Tokio task dials the original destination (5 s budget, `DIAL_TIMEOUT`); on success the smoltcp thread allocates the mapped port, creates the listen socket, injects the buffered SYN, and holds the pre-connected stream for the bridge task at promotion; on failure/timeout it sends a crafted RST (seq=0, ack=SYN seq + 1 + payload bytes, correct IP/TCP checksums) through the thread's outbound sequence. Retransmitted SYNs for a held dial are dropped (duplicate-dial race guarded in the DialSucceeded handler); stale held entries (>30 s) start a fresh dial; pending dials capped at 1024; client RST cancels a held dial; pre-connected streams are dropped if the handshake never completes. `tcp_bridge_task` no longer dials — it takes the pre-connected stream. Tests: 5 new (refused → RST, never SYN-ACK; retransmitted SYN dials once; dial budget enforced via `dial_with_timeout`; RST fields/checksums; SYN seq/payload parsing); one existing test (`tcp_outbound_frames_carry_session_connection_id`) updated to bind a real listener, since unconditional SYN-ACK was the old behavior. Full server suite: 97 passed / 4 failed — the 4 are the pre-existing sandbox UDP set (2 server_integration relay + 2 wireguard), same as baseline. Timeout-branch gap, stated openly: no hanging destination exists in this sandbox (loopback connects complete synchronously, non-loopback is proxy-instant or EPERM-fast, iptables blocked), so the end-to-end timeout → RST path is covered by the `dial_with_timeout` unit test plus the refused test's shared DialFailed → RST path, not by a live blackhole dial. Deployed 2026-11-04 ~00:06 EST at the user's word: binary SHA 492b8cfe…, image b5451a70ed09, compose backup docker-compose.yml.bak-2026-11-04-dialfirst, rollback = re-point compose at qr + up -d. Verified: container binary SHA matches the build, listeners 8080/8081/8082 TCP + 51820 UDP, health 200, status live, 2 sessions + 20 TCP flows re-established ~1 min after restart (QUIC reconnect-churn warnings in logs only). Open: live phone-side check (phone SYN to 10.8.0.1:853 must RST after ~5 s, never SYN-ACK); device bench; GitHub push. |
| 4.29 | Single required dns_server for tunnel DNS (blind-forward only) | DEPLOYED to Dell (localhost/bonded-server:dnsrequired, 2026-11-04) | User direction 2026-11-04: the tunnel DNS proxy takes exactly one operator-configured upstream and blind-forwards queries verbatim to it; no /etc/resolv.conf fallback, no gateway guessing, no public default, no resolver list. Operator redundancy belongs in their own local resolver (dnsmasq/unbound/Pi-hole), pointed to by dns_server. New required server config `dns_server` (bare IP defaults to port 53; IPv6 rejected, tunnel DNS is IPv4-only); server fails to start when missing/empty/unparseable, with env override BONDED_DNS_SERVER. `DnsProxy` holds one SocketAddr; `system_resolvers()` and `parse_resolv_conf()` are deleted; the address is threaded through SmoltcpForwarder::new into all four session paths (NaiveTCP, WSS bootstrap, QUIC, WireGuard) via BootstrapContext plus explicit args. Root cause it closes: the container's /etc/resolv.conf pointed at the systemd stub 127.0.0.53, which will not forward single-label names to unicast DNS, so Chrome's absolute http://cw-r10.:4096/ timed out/SERVFAILed while multi-label names worked; the router 192.168.1.1 answers the bare single-label name directly. Dell config set to dns_server = "192.168.1.1" (compose + server.toml backups .bak-2026-11-04-dnsrequired; rollback = re-point compose at ipv6gate + up, old binary ignores the new field). Verified: core 44/44, server 98 passed / 4 failed (pre-existing sandbox UDP set; removed resolv.conf parsing test accounts for 99 -> 98); release binary SHA 624223ac, image 88db16253420, health 200, status live with 1 session re-established and a TCP flow flowing within a minute, binary SHA inside the container matches, listeners 8080/8081/8082 TCP + 51820 UDP, clean startup. Open: live-phone Chrome retry of http://cw-r10.:4096/ against the new server; GitHub push (commits local only). |
| 4.30 | Remove explicit gateway 853 RST (revert of 4.27's special case) | Implemented locally, NOT deployed | User direction 2026-11-04: just remove the RST. The 4.27 explicit rejection (PollCommand::RejectSyn, dispatch_tcp special case for 10.8.0.1:853, TUN_DOT_PORT) is deleted; an 853 SYN now goes through dial-first like any other destination: held, dialed, RST only on dial failure/timeout (DIAL_TIMEOUT, since the gateway route blackholes), never SYN-ACKed. emit_syn_rst stays for the DialFailed path. Rationale: the immediate RST was chase complexity from the DNS investigation — Chrome's failure was UDP to the stub with zero TCP DNS attempts in the net log, so it never helped DNS. Dial-first itself is kept. Test gateway_dot_syn_is_held_for_dial replaces the old immediate-RST test. Server 98 passed / 4 pre-existing sandbox UDP fails; core 44/44. Dell still runs dnsrequired (with the immediate RST) until this is deployed; rollback of this change alone would be a redeploy of dnsrequired. |
